# Seed 57 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - small at 64 bits, **not small at both widths**: the first run selected for speed at 32 bits (Iteration 378)

`seed 57 tag2 12h widths then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 6 --widths 8,4 --frontier --cpus 2,8`,
commit d1d1a44e6 (Iteration 355) - the after-seed-56 batch: selection at both widths (--widths 8,4), the
JIT genes jitimg and jitvar, the comparison's 32-bit section. The chain 14:46 Oct 17 - 02:38 Oct 18 UTC
(11 h 52 min): the evolution 8 h 22 min (15:43-00:05), the re-measure 26 min, the comparison at 6 rounds
and both widths 2 h 07 min. 32,567 designs, generations 0-128: 24,104 alive (23,480 with a 32-bit speed),
8,333 screened (27.0% from generation 5), 130 refused before running (the reach limit at scale 0).

**The verdict.** At 64 bits, the five measures: fastest +3.9%, smallest +0.00%, memory +0 KB, hypervolume
+2.0%, best Q +1.2% - small. At 32 bits: its fastest 0.337 of s6 (1a3cfe3e03), **+20.3%** against every
earlier run's best (seed 56's 0.405) - so by the rule the owner confirmed at Iteration 376 (a run
selected at both widths is small only if its fastest at 32 bits is under 5% too), **not small**: the
first run judged by it, applied by hand (its comparison ran on d1d1a44e6, before the rule). By the old
rule it would have been the fifth small run in a row. Its 64-bit fastest, 40a30d6da8 (0.128), is 0.384
at 32 bits - itself ahead of every earlier run's 32-bit best. The Q-best of all runs: this run's
9d47925af2, speed 0.132 at 31,871 bytes, 164 KB - Q 0.213 of s6's.

**The facts** (seed-facts.py): the front 200 designs (mutation 143, borrowed 23, crossover 13, carried 21;
via the attic 6), 198 with a 32-bit speed; speed against 32-bit speed over the front, Spearman 0.717 -
the two objectives apart, the front now holding 32-bit specialists: the fastest at 32 bits, 1a3cfe3e03,
is 0.332 at 64 bits. Children: mutation 53.6%, crossover 34.7%, borrowed 11.7%; via the attic 4,926. The
JIT on 115 of 200; clang 46; regpin 62 (1 of them 3); bfind 85; jitimg 5; at 0 on every design of the
front: hotcalls, rtloopall, rtiplus, sysc, tailpn, alignhot, jitvar - jitvar never taken in its first
run. Format-10 words: ?BRANCH8 200, (LOOP) 199, I 196, +! 193, (+LOOP) 173, HASH 161, SKIP 146, PLACE 146,
SCAN 134, FILL 134, (PARSE) 134, (DO) 132, 2@ 126, THREAD-FIND 107.

**Held out** (heldout.py): sieve over the 200, Spearman 0.946 with the selection speed (seeds 53-56: 0.948,
0.953, 0.959, 0.963) - steady; the 8th fastest by selection ranks 101 on sieve, the selection's most
flattered 2d7ce59336 (2.71x slower held out than selected).

**No predictions** were written for this run (nor seed 56's) - results/predictions.md; seed 58's are.

**The checks - all four gathered** (results/xcheck-seed57.md), of seed 56's front: dream 64-bit Spearman
0.980; at 32 bits 0.713 and the same designs at 64 bits 0.949 - the width's skew again; the Tegra 0.881.

---

# Evolved VM designs

24234 designs evaluated, 24104 alive, 8333 screened - not measured (Iteration 200). Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).


The Q-best design (binary + image x run time x memory - the knee; information): e6b5f1749e, speed 0.139, binary + image 27,944, memory 160 KB - Q 0.185 of s6-cv8b's.

## The front's genomes

Each design above, and any other the front was measured again with, as one JSON line: its record without the raw timings. A tool that takes a database (--db) reads this report as one - the lines that are not JSON are skipped - so results/ is all a new chat needs to rebuild a front design.
